Method. 41 first-pass auditors (one per component, the four largest split by document) read their documents in full, resolved every path, symbol, link and anchor, and verified the most consequential behavioural claims against source. A second, independent pass of 37 refuters then tried to knock down every finding. Second pass: 174 confirmed, 46 partial, 14 refuted.

Mechanical gate. tests/test_docs_accuracy.py passes (61 tests) and the corpus inventory validates a full-coverage ledger; everything here is semantic and would not be caught by that gate.

Caveat. The working tree was edited while the audit ran (save-conflict work in frontend/src/hooks/usePipelineAPI.ts, src/haute/routes/_save_pipeline.py and the frontend-shared / server-api specs), so findings in those two components should be re-read against the current files before acting.

Findings

F002 P1 accuracy confirmed git-integration specs/git-integration/low-level.md:105
**`GitWorkingBranchResponse.state`** (computed by `working_branch_status`) is one of six literal values: `"no-repository"` ... `"divergent"` ... or `"ready"`.

The readiness route contract has seven states, not six. The spec omits `"git-unavailable"`, the state returned when no git binary is on PATH — a value the frontend explicitly branches on. An engineer implementing or consuming `GET /api/git/working-branch` from this spec would treat an unhandled value as impossible.

Evidence and fix

Evidence src/haute/schemas.py:2828-2836 defines `GitWorkingBranchState = Literal["git-unavailable", "no-repository", "unset", "detached", "invalid", "divergent", "ready"]` (seven). src/haute/_git_read_models.py:66-71 returns `GitWorkingBranchResponse(state="git-unavailable", ...)` when `git_binary_available()` is false. frontend/src/api/types.ts:1453 and frontend/src/components/BranchIndicator.tsx:166 handle that state; tests/test_git_engine.py:640 asserts it.

Second pass specs/git-integration/low-level.md:105 says the state "is one of six literal values" and enumerates six; src/haute/schemas.py:2828-2836 defines GitWorkingBranchState with seven literals including "git-unavailable", and src/haute/_git_read_models.py:66-71 returns state="git-unavailable" when git_binary_available() is false. The table row at line 116 repeats "six-state readiness contract", so the route contract is stated wrongly in two places.

Suggested fix Change to seven literal values and document `"git-unavailable"` (no git binary on PATH; distinct from `"no-repository"` so the UI does not offer init), in both this paragraph and the `GET /api/git/working-branch` table row.

F003 P1 accuracy confirmed hosted-project-storage specs/hosted-project-storage/low-level.md:43
It then hands the rest to `BindTask` (one background thread, one bind at a time) and returns **202** with `outcome: "pending"`.

The bind route returns HTTP 200, not 202. The response body's `outcome` is indeed the literal "pending", but the status code stated in the spec is wrong, and it is stated in bold as a route contract an integrator would code against.

Evidence and fix

Evidence src/haute/routes/git.py:596 declares `@router.post("/storage/bind", response_model=GitBindStorageResponse)` with no `status_code=` argument, so FastAPI returns 200; `grep -n 202 src/haute/routes/git.py tests/test_storage_routes.py` finds no 202 anywhere in the route module or its tests.

Second pass specs/hosted-project-storage/low-level.md:43 states the bind route "returns **202** with `outcome: \"pending\"`". src/haute/routes/git.py:596 is `@router.post("/storage/bind", response_model=GitBindStorageResponse)` with no `status_code=`, so FastAPI returns 200; no `202` appears anywhere in the module. The body literal is right (src/haute/schemas.py:2937 `outcome: Literal["pending"] = "pending"`), so only the status code is wrong — but it is stated in bold as a route contract.

Suggested fix Either change the sentence to "returns 200 with `outcome: \"pending\"`", or add `status_code=202` to the route decorator and pin it in tests/test_storage_routes.py.

F004 P1 accuracy confirmed hosted-project-storage specs/hosted-project-storage/low-level.md:45
A `uc://` URL first acquires the claim (a live foreign holder → 409 with the holder record; the UI offers bind-elsewhere or fork), then branches on whether `HEAD.json` exists under the location

The 409-on-claimed-location contract is stale from the pre-async bind design. Since the route now returns before any network work, a claimed `uc://` location cannot produce a 409 response; the failure is reported through the readiness response's `storage_bind` record instead. The same false claim appears twice more in this document.

Evidence and fix

Evidence src/haute/routes/git.py:596-627 (`git_bind_storage`) calls only `precheck_bind` and `bind_task().start(...)`; `precheck_bind` (src/haute/_project_storage.py) does URL validation, the already-bound refusal and the state-volume check only — it never touches the claim. `StorageClaimedError` does not appear anywhere in src/haute/routes/git.py, and the only 409s in that module are the milestone fork gate (line 289) and the push rejection (line 583). The claim is acquired inside the background `BindTask`, so the holder surfaces asynchronously on `storage_bind` (`GitStorageBind.claim`), never as a 409 from the bind call.

Second pass src/haute/routes/git.py:596-627 calls only `precheck_bind` (src/haute/_project_storage.py:824-846 — URL validation, already-bound refusal, state-volume check; it never touches the claim) and `bind_task().start(...)`, then returns. `StorageClaimedError` does not appear in routes/git.py at all; it is caught in `BindTask._run` (src/haute/_project_storage.py:640) and turned into `self._fail(url, str(exc), claim=exc.claim)`, i.e. a failed `storage_bind` record. So the "→ 409" on line 45 (and the same claim on lines 11 and 37) describes a contract the async route cannot produce.

Suggested fix Delete the "→ 409" from line 45, from the Module map entry for `src/haute/routes/git.py` (line 11: "409 with the structured holder when the location is claimed"), and from the `StorageClaimedError` bullet (line 37: "→ HTTP 409 at bind"); replace with "surfaces as a failed `storage_bind` carrying the holder record".

F006 P1 accuracy confirmed sandbox-security specs/sandbox-security/high-level.md:212
Haute has no reverse-proxy or shared-host mode because this UI can execute project code and access project files.

A reverse-proxied, multi-user hosted deployment mode exists in the working tree and is the one place where this component's entire local-session gate is turned off. The security spec asserts the opposite as a hard product boundary, so a reader of the sandbox-security corpus never learns that a shipped code path disables the middleware this document is written about.

Evidence and fix

Evidence src/haute/hosted.py:1-20 module docstring: "Hosted-mode support: run haute behind a platform SSO reverse proxy... A hosted platform such as Databricks Apps inverts that boundary". src/haute/hosted.py:125-155 `create_app()` sets `os.environ[DISABLE_AUTH_ENV] = "1"` before importing `haute.server` and returns `PlatformProxyBoundary(server_app)`, which rewrites the Host header to loopback and strips `Forwarded`/`X-Forwarded-*` (src/haute/hosted.py:104-123) so `LocalTrustedHostMiddleware` accepts platform-proxied traffic. specs/hosted-databricks-app/low-level.md documents this component.

Second pass specs/sandbox-security/high-level.md:211-213 reads "Haute has no reverse-proxy or shared-host mode because this UI can execute project code and access project files." src/haute/hosted.py:1-21 documents exactly such a mode ("run haute behind a platform SSO reverse proxy"), and hosted.py:125-155 create_app() sets os.environ[DISABLE_AUTH_ENV]="1" then wraps the server app in PlatformProxyBoundary, which (hosted.py:107-122) strips Forwarded/X-Forwarded-*/Host and injects a 127.0.0.1 Host so LocalTrustedHostMiddleware accepts platform traffic. specs/hosted-databricks-app/{high,low}-level.md exist; the sandbox-security corpus never mentions hosted mode (grep for "hosted" across both files returns nothing). Security-boundary assertion contradicted by shipped code.

Suggested fix Restate the boundary as it is: loopback-only is the posture of `haute serve`/the stock app, and the only exception is the explicit hosted deployment entry point in `src/haute/hosted.py`, which delegates authentication to a platform SSO proxy — cross-link ../hosted-databricks-app/high-level.md.

F007 P1 stale confirmed server-api specs/server-api/low-level.md:601
Pipeline-list parse failures surface `str(exc)` as `PipelineSummary.error` only for `ParseError` (hand-authored, path-safe messages); any other exception is logged server-side and surfaced as a fixed "Failed to parse pipeline. Check the server logs for details." message

`PipelineSummary` has no `error` field at all, and the model is `extra="forbid"`, so the described field cannot exist. There is also no `ParseError`-specific branch and no such fixed message anywhere in the source. The paragraph describes a `GET /api/pipelines` response contract the code does not implement, and contradicts this same document's own endpoint table (line 181).

Evidence and fix

Evidence src/haute/schemas.py:1310-1318 defines `PipelineSummary` with `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")` and exactly `name, description, file, node_count, load_status, diagnostic_count` — no `error`. src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:586-613 `_parse_one` catches a bare `Exception` and returns `PipelineSummary(..., load_status="source_only", diagnostic_count=1)`. `grep -rn "Failed to parse pipeline" src/haute/ --include=*.py` returns nothing.

Second pass Quote is verbatim but lives at specs/server-api/low-level.md:613-616, not 601 (601 is the TraceCorrelationUnsupportedError row). Substance holds: src/haute/schemas.py PipelineSummary is ConfigDict(extra="forbid") with only name/description/file/node_count/load_status/diagnostic_count; src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:586-613 _parse_one catches bare Exception and returns load_status="source_only", diagnostic_count=1; grep for "Failed to parse pipeline" in src/haute returns nothing. Public GET /api/pipelines contract described wrongly.

Suggested fix Replace the paragraph with the shipped behaviour: any load failure for a discovered file yields a `source_only` summary with `diagnostic_count=1` and the failure is logged server-side; there is no per-item error string on the wire.

F008 P1 stale confirmed submodels specs/submodels/high-level.md:373
Depends on the codegen component through `SavePipelineService`: create emits the rewritten parent plus submodel files; dissolve uses `graph_to_code` only when no submodels remain and `graph_to_code_multi` when other occurrences remain.

Both submodel-mutating routes are transform-only and call no codegen at all. This bullet asserts that create writes the parent and submodel files, contradicting the same document's Behaviour section ('It does not write the parent, module, config, or sidecar') and the code.

Evidence and fix

Evidence src/haute/routes/submodel.py contains no reference to graph_to_code or graph_to_code_multi (grep over the file returns only ConfigError/ParseError imports at line 18). create_submodel (src/haute/routes/submodel.py:118-214) only parses, transforms via create_submodel_graph, calls svc.validate_new_module_files (a read-only no-clobber check, src/haute/routes/_save_pipeline.py:475-477) and returns a response; dissolve_submodel (src/haute/routes/submodel.py:295-358) only calls flatten_graph and returns.

there is no cancel endpoint because the underlying blocking shred is not cooperatively cancellable.

The stated rationale is contradicted by the code: the structured-input build is explicitly cooperatively cancellable. The absence of a cancel *route* is true; the reason given is not. `specs/caching/low-level.md:123` repeats the same framing and line 118-119 describes the work only as "Blocking shred work runs with a response timeout", omitting the isolated-worker/cancellation-gate machinery entirely.

The shred work is therefore cooperatively cancellable; only the absence of a cancel route is true.

Evidence src/haute/routes/json_cache.py:640-674 — `exc.kind == "source_changed"` raises `HTTPException(status_code=409, detail="The structured source changed during the cache build; retry the build.")`, `exc.kind == "memory"` raises 507, `IsolatedWorkerMemoryLimitExceededError`/`IsolatedWorkerMemoryLimitUnsupportedError` raise 507 with `_isolated_memory_detail(...)` (json_cache.py:263-288), `IsolatedWorkerCrashedError` with `terminal_reason == "memory_limited"` raises 507, and `IsolatedWorkerStoppedError` raises 409.

Both status families are live and unspecced.

Suggested fix Add the 507 memory-limit family (worker RSS limit, native cap unavailable, memory-limited crash, admission rejection) and the 409 source-changed/worker-stopped responses to the Failure model and to the low-level Error handling and boundary failure ordering.

Consulting only `working/` (the volatile layer) reported `cached=False` whenever it was absent or stale-fingerprinted while `committed/` — the durable layer that survives a restart — was valid and still serving every run, inviting a rebuild of a cache already in use. F095 P2 accuracy confirmed hosted-databricks-app specs/hosted-databricks-app/high-level.md:39
For HTTP and WebSocket traffic, the boundary records a non-empty `X-Forwarded-Email` on the ASGI scope

The boundary applies no non-emptiness test. Any present `X-Forwarded-Email` header, including one with an empty or whitespace value, is decoded and written to the scope key, so a blank identity is recorded as a user. The low-level invariant at line 32 ("absent rather than empty when the proxy sends no identity, so consumers cannot mistake 'no identity' for a blank user") states the same guarantee and is equally contradicted; consumers such as `src/haute/routes/git.py:614` (`bound_by`) and `src/haute/server.py:518` (log `user`) receive the empty string.

Evidence and fix

Evidence src/haute/hosted.py:112-121 — the loop sets `forwarded_user = value.decode("latin-1")` for any header named `x-forwarded-email`, and the scope key is written under `if forwarded_user is not None`, so `b""` yields `scope[FORWARDED_USER_SCOPE_KEY] == ""`. tests/test_hosted.py:124-133 covers only the populated header and the wholly absent header; no empty-value case exists.

Second pass specs/hosted-databricks-app/high-level.md:39-40 reads "the boundary records a non-empty `X-Forwarded-Email` on the ASGI scope" and low-level.md:32 asserts the key is "absent rather than empty". src/haute/hosted.py:112-121 records `forwarded_user = value.decode("latin-1")` for any header named x-forwarded-email and writes the scope key under `if forwarded_user is not None`, so a present-but-empty header yields "". Consumers src/haute/routes/git.py:614 (bound_by) and 668 (forked_by) and src/haute/server.py:518 then persist/log a blank identity; tests/test_hosted.py has no empty-value case (only "someone@example.com" at line 35).

Suggested fix Either state the shipped behaviour ("records the `X-Forwarded-Email` value when the header is present") in both documents, or, if the emptiness guard is the intended contract, add the guard in `src/haute/hosted.py` plus a test and keep the current wording.

A partial or whitespace-only hosted environment fails at startup and names the missing contract variables without exposing their values.

A whitespace-only environment does not fail at startup. Values are stripped before the presence test, so when all three variables hold only whitespace nothing is present and detection returns `None` — the ordinary local posture — rather than raising. Only a genuinely partial set raises.

Evidence and fix

Evidence src/haute/hosted.py:74-84 — `values = {name: (env.get(name) or "").strip() ...}`; `if not present: return None` precedes the missing-variable `RuntimeError`. tests/test_hosted.py:78-79 `test_whitespace_values_count_as_absent` asserts `databricks_app_environment(environ=dict.fromkeys(_CONTRACT, " ")) is None`.

F204 P2 accuracy partial json-shredding specs/json-shredding/low-level.md:216
3. No-op trapdoor: if `is_per_port_cache_valid` already holds for the current in-memory schema and on-disk data file, return the existing `meta.json` payload without rebuilding. 4. Still under the lock, record the data-file signature (`_data_file_signature`) *before* reading records. 5. Still under the lock, build the shared `_EmittingTableSpec`s once

The stated step order does not match the code: the table specs, schema fingerprint and the data-file signature are all computed *before* the no-op validity check, and the check consumes the already-computed signature. F117 P2 accuracy confirmed pipeline-config specs/pipeline-config/low-level.md:41

`submodel()` cannot be called as `Pipeline.submodel(file)` — `definition_id`, `instance_id` and `alias` are required keyword-only arguments — and it records more than the path: it appends a `RegisteredSubmodel` occurrence record and enforces alias/instance-id uniqueness and node-name conflicts.

F128 P2 missing confirmed sandbox-security specs/sandbox-security/low-level.md:343
## Error handling

Two exception types this component raises are absent from Error handling (and from Key types): `ArtifactVersionMismatchError`, a `HauteError` subclass raised by the pickle/joblib load path, and `ExecutionError`, raised by `_exec_user_code`. Both are described nowhere in the section that claims to enumerate "the exception types raised, where they propagate to"; `ArtifactVersionMismatchError` is only mentioned in passing in Control flow.
